# Third-party notices

Clew itself is © J. McKenzie Alexander and released under the **GNU
General Public License, version 3 or later** — see `LICENSE`.

It ships, and is built on, the work of many other people. What follows is
every third-party component distributed with Clew, its licence, and that
licence's own text. MIT and ISC — which between them account for most of
this list — require the copyright notice to travel with the software, so
reproducing the text is not politeness but the condition of use.

_Generated by `node scripts/gen-notices.js`. Re-run it whenever a runtime
dependency is added, removed or upgraded._

**476 components.**

## Components carrying obligations beyond attribution

### citeproc — `CPAL-1.0 OR AGPL-1.0`

The CSL citation processor behind Clew's bibliography, reached through
`jmarkdown → citation-js → citeproc`. It is **dual-licensed**: a user may
rely on either the Common Public Attribution License 1.0 or the GNU Affero
GPL 1.0.

It is not linked into Clew's own code. It ships as an unpacked file tree
under `Resources/engine/` and executes inside the forked jmarkdown worker —
a separate process Clew communicates with over IPC.

CPAL-1.0 carries an **attribution requirement**; this notices file is part
of how that is met. Both options additionally treat network deployment as
distribution, which a desktop application does not trigger — but anyone
putting Clew or its engine behind a network service should read both
licences before doing so.

### Fonts — `OFL-1.1`

SIL Open Font License. Reserved font names may not be reused, and the fonts
may not be sold on their own. Bundling them in an application is expressly
permitted.

### Components shipping no licence file

These declare a licence in their manifest but ship no licence text. The
declared identifier is reproduced; the canonical text is the standard text
of that licence.

# Attribution

Attribution is required by MIT, SIL OFL, and CC BY licenses. Downloaded Font
Awesome Free files already contain embedded comments with sufficient
attribution, so you shouldn't need to do anything additional when using these
files normally.

We've kept attribution comments terse, so we ask that you do not actively work
to remove them from files, especially code. They're a great way for folks to
learn about Font Awesome.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
